**Three hundred and twenty-seventh move (Chapter Eleven Part B §8.1.2 and §8.2.2: the LEQU-split sentence rewritten; 2026-11-06; wording, with one meaning shift flagged).** Decision (custodian, asked in session: the sentence "Do not split LEQU into separate scores for ecological, system, or resource benefits, unless the adopted implementation needs that breakdown to make audits transparent" and its violation-side mirror "are confusing", suggested "each relevant category is assessed separately, but their total enters the record in both. That way we don't need the traceability disclaimer"; uncommitted). §8.1.2 now reads "Assess each relevant kind of benefit (ecological, system, or resource) separately, then enter one LEQU total for the whole contribution in the record." §8.2.2 reads "Assess each relevant kind of harm (ecological harm, system harm, or resource loss) separately, then enter one LEQU total for the whole violation in the record." The three named kinds were kept so the rule does not widen to every benefit or harm route. What stays: one LEQU total is the only measurement that enters the record, so the old ban on parallel scores holds. What moves: (1) the audit exception is gone, because the category-by-category working is now part of the assessment itself and needs no exception to exist; (2) assessing each relevant kind separately is now stated as the way to reach the total, where before it was only permitted when audits needed it. Nothing else cites the old sentence, and no other file used its wording. The obligation inventory matches 59 and 59, but it cannot see this change (neither sentence carries a modal word), so the by-hand reading is the check. Generated files were regenerated. No threshold, clock, authority, or scope changed. Translations were not touched.

**Three hundred and twenty-eighth move (Chapter Eleven Part B §9 canonical descriptor rule rewritten for average readers; stale cite repaired; 2026-11-06; wording and one pointer).** Decision (custodian, asked in session: "please rewrite for average humans"; uncommitted). The bold lead-in is now **Descriptor rule.** ("Canonical" was jargon, and nothing else cites the old label). The opening cite "Sections 4.3–4.4 own the normalized descriptor catalog" was stale pre-split numbering (the old §4.3 and §4.4 are the two route-descriptor catalogs, now §9.1 and §9.2); it is now two named links, [§9.1 Contribution route descriptor catalog and measurement roles] and [§9.2 Violation route descriptor catalog and measurement roles], "are the official list of descriptors for both axes." The other sentences were put in everyday words: a descriptor says **how** the verified benefit or harm happened; descriptors stack and none excludes another, and one standing record may carry several where the verified facts warrant; they are subordinate to the applicable slot, glossed as "they add detail to it and never replace it"; the catalog is "a starting point, not a closed list"; implementations may use stable identifiers (glossed as fixed IDs of their own) mapped to the catalog but must not create a conflicting taxonomy whose categories are exclusive, so that a record can carry only one (the "conflicting exclusive taxonomy" prohibition spelled out, not widened: it still needs both the conflict and the one-category exclusivity); before the facts are verified an alleged descriptor may support lawful evidence preservation, sorting the case (triage), or temporary safeguards; only a verified descriptor may be recorded as a Question 2 output or affect standing. Same modal verbs as before (may, must not, may). Obligation inventory for the file: 59 and 59, matched one to one at 0.5 and 0.3; at the default 0.75 it flags two clauses on vocabulary alone (the three glossed words "stackable", "non-exclusive", "subordinate", and the added gloss "fixed IDs of their own"), and compared by hand they carry the same duties. Generated files were regenerated. No duty, authority, threshold, clock, or scope changed. Follow-up (custodian: "Much better. Now please add bullets"): after the two opening sentences (the two catalogs are the official list; a descriptor says how) the paragraph became a lead-in, "These rules apply:", and six bullets, one per remaining sentence in the same order and the same words: descriptors stack; they are subordinate to the applicable slot; the catalog is a starting point; identifiers mapped to the catalog but no conflicting exclusive taxonomy; an alleged descriptor may support preservation, sorting, or temporary safeguards; only a verified descriptor may be recorded or affect standing. The obligation inventory still matches 59 and 59 at 0.5 and 0.3 (at 0.75 it flags only the first bullet, because the tool reads the lead-in sentence with it). No sentence was added or dropped apart from the lead-in. Second follow-up (custodian: "please clarify" the second and third bullets): the second bullet now reads "They rank below the slot: the slot (1–9) says how big the benefit or harm was, and descriptors add detail about how it happened but never replace it," naming what the slot measures instead of calling descriptors "subordinate to the applicable slot"; it still does not say descriptors never move the slot. The third bullet now reads "The listed descriptors are a base, not the full set; the catalog does not rule out other kinds of benefit or harm," in place of "a starting point, not a closed list"; it states that the catalog is not exhaustive and adds no permission to add descriptors and no new rule (how implementations may tie their own identifiers to the catalog stays with the next bullet). Obligation inventory 59 and 59, matched at 0.5 and 0.3; the default 0.75 flags only the first bullet, which the tool reads together with the lead-in, on vocabulary. Translations were not touched.

**Three hundred and thirty-sixth move (Chapter Eleven Part B §9.2.4: bare "designation" qualified in the Accountability Obstruction Misconduct cell; 2026-11-07; wording and one link).** Decision (custodian): the 335th move left "designation" bare, and it is neither a defined term nor qualified. Finding: the corpus defines the process, not the outcome. The defined term is Anti-Constitutional Misconduct Review (Chapter Five, Accountability band file), and the outcome is written as "anti-constitutional-misconduct designation", "Chapter Thirteen designation" or "final designation"; Chapter Thirteen itself uses the bare word only as shorthand inside its own headings. The convention is therefore to qualify, not to add a new definition. The cell now ends: "so it can be sent to [Anti-Constitutional Misconduct Review](core_05.035_band_accountability.md#anti-constitutional-misconduct-review) in Chapter Thirteen. Being sent there is not the same as being designated for anti-constitutional misconduct: Chapter Thirteen decides that separately." The other uses of "designation" in `core_11.005` already carry "Chapter Thirteen" or "anti-constitutional" and were left alone. Verification: obligation inventory for `core_11.005`, 60 and 60, matched one to one at 0.75, 0.5 and 0.3 against a snapshot taken before this move. **Three hundred and thirty-seventh move (bare "designation" added to the lexical-vocabulary audit; 33 lines qualified in 15 files; 2026-11-07; vocabulary rule, wording only).** Decision (custodian): add "designation" to the family of rules that reject an unqualified noun. The word has several unrelated senses (the Chapter Thirteen anti-constitutional-misconduct designation, a backup designation of a forum or record authority named in a System Charter or Institutional Charter, an emergency designation in Chapter Fourteen, a hostile designation of a state or organization under Article XIV-D (Counter-Espionage Limits), a designation of accountable incident authority), so "the designation" alone does not say which. (1) **Rule.** `avoid-bare-designation` in `tools/lexical_vocabulary_audit.py`, modelled on `avoid-bare-placement`: the noun is rejected after a determiner, preposition, conjunction, or one of the generic adjectives final, corresponding, matching, additional, separate, and existing; at the start of a sentence, bullet, or cell; and as a `designation-...` prefix compound. "Designation of ...", "designation under ...", and any qualified form (final anti-constitutional-misconduct designation, backup designation) stay. The scan skips this move log (AUDIT-SCOPE-01) and the two Chapter Thirteen files, where the chapter title names the kind; the exemption is a scope choice the custodian can narrow, and it leaves about seventy bare uses inside those two files unchanged. An internal regression check, the rule text in the report, and an entry in `tools/architecture/lexical_guardrails.json` were added. (2) **Existing uses.** The first run found 33 lines. Each now says which designation: "anti-constitutional-misconduct designation" for the Chapter Thirteen outcome (`core_00`, `core_05.035`, `core_06.005`, `core_07`, `core_11.000`, `core_11.005`, `core_11.010`, `core_12`, `core_14`, `core_16`, and `ci_00.210`); "backup designation" where a System Charter, Institutional Charter, or designation names a backup route (`core_11.000` two lines, `cf_14.415` two lines, `cf_14.435`); "emergency designation" in `cf_14.445`; and "hostile designation" in `core_06.010`. Where a sentence repeated "that designation" it now says "it" or repeats the full term, and "designation-only review" became "review limited to the anti-constitutional-misconduct designation". Two glosses in Chapter Five that quote the section's topic were retitled to match. The meaning of three phrases was read from context and is flagged for the custodian: "designation" in "System Charter, Institutional Charter, or designation names" is taken as a backup designation, "emergency filing or designation" as an emergency designation, and "each designation must be reviewed" in Article XIV-D as a hostile designation. Verification: obligation inventory across the 15 edited files, 1817 and 1817, matched one to one at 0.75, 0.5 and 0.3 against a snapshot taken before the edits. No duty, authority, threshold, clock, or scope of any rule changed. Translations were not touched.

**Three hundred and thirty-eighth move (bare "designation" rule now applies inside Chapter Thirteen; 66 uses qualified in two files; 2026-11-07; vocabulary rule and wording only).** Decision (custodian): remove the Chapter Thirteen exemption that the 337th move gave `avoid-bare-designation`. (1) **Rule.** `core_13.000` and `core_13.005` are scanned like every other file; only this move log stays exempt (AUDIT-SCOPE-01). The internal regression check now expects a finding in Chapter Thirteen, and the guardrails entry and the rule comment no longer mention the exemption. The 337th entry said a `designation-...` prefix compound is always flagged; the scan treats the compound as the noun, so it is flagged only after a determiner, preposition, conjunction, generic adjective, or at the start of a sentence, bullet, or cell, and the rule text now says so. (2) **Chapter Thirteen text.** The scan found 65 lines with 66 uses (Part A about 45 lines, Part B 20 lines). Each now says "anti-constitutional-misconduct designation", the form Chapter Thirteen §3, Chapter Five, and the other chapters already use: "after final designation" is "after final anti-constitutional-misconduct designation", "the matching designation" is "the matching anti-constitutional-misconduct designation", "designation criteria" is "anti-constitutional-misconduct designation criteria", and the repeated "an additional designation criterion" and "the existing designation criteria" in Part B follow the same pattern. The sentence-initial "Designation review runs alongside that Chapter Twelve work" became "Anti-constitutional-misconduct designation review runs alongside that Chapter Twelve work". (3) **Titles.** The Part A title "ANTI-CONSTITUTIONAL MISCONDUCT — DESIGNATION" lost its dash and now reads "ANTI-CONSTITUTIONAL MISCONDUCT DESIGNATION", because the word after the dash stood bare; its explicit anchor is unchanged, but the heading-derived fragment lost its double hyphen (`#chapter-thirteen-part-a-anti-constitutional-misconduct--designation` became `#chapter-thirteen-part-a-anti-constitutional-misconduct-designation`), so seven links in `core_06.015`, `core_07`, `core_11.000`, `core_12`, and `core_13.005` were retargeted; the translation drafts under `project/translation_drafts/` still carry the old fragment and were not touched. The §2 heading is now "Criteria set (for anti-constitutional-misconduct designation) — Accountability at constitutional scale". No other file quotes either heading text; the pages index and the plain-terms edition regenerate from them. Flagged for the custodian: the sentences are longer and repeat the full term, which reads heavily in places; "ACM" (used in nine places in Part A) was not touched and is not defined in Part B. Verification: obligation inventory for the two files, 62 and 62, matched one to one at 0.75, 0.5 and 0.3 against a snapshot taken before the edits. No duty, authority, threshold, clock, or scope changed. Translations were not touched.

**Three hundred and thirty-ninth move (ACMD and ACM adopted as defined abbreviations in Chapter Thirteen and Chapter Twelve; 2026-11-07; wording and rule text only).** Decision (custodian): "ACM or ACMD is fine to use in chapter 13, or on repeated use in another chapter." ACM stands for anti-constitutional misconduct, and ACMD for anti-constitutional-misconduct designation (the 338th move spelled the full term out about seventy times in Chapter Thirteen). (1) **Definitions.** Each abbreviation is defined in parentheses at its first use in each reading unit: ACMD in the Part A file-structure block, the Part A reader-guidance block, and the Part A body opening; in the Part B file-structure block and body opening; and in the Chapter Twelve file-structure block and at its first body use (the step that routes a qualifying slot 7–9 finding to Chapter Thirteen). ACM, which Part A already used nine times without a definition, is now defined in the Part A file-structure block, the reader-guidance block, and the body opening. (2) **Conversion.** In `core_13.000`, `core_13.005`, and `core_12.000`, about 75 uses of the full term became ACMD; the defining uses (two or three per file) and the headings, link text, glosses, and plain-terms lines stay spelled out (12 spelled-out uses remain in Part A, 2 in Part B, and 2 in Chapter Twelve). Headings, link text, `(*gloss*)` text, and the "In plain terms" lines keep the full term, so a reader of those never meets the abbreviation undefined, and the definition is repeated in each collapsed block that uses it; "designation criteria" and "designation boundary" in the Part A body opening became "ACMD criteria" and "ACMD boundary". (3) **Rule.** `avoid-bare-designation` already passes both abbreviations, because it matches only the word "designation"; the rule comment, the report text, the guardrails entry, and the internal regression check now say so. Not done, by choice: other chapters that repeat the full term (Chapter Five 15 times, Chapter Fourteen 10, Chapter Sixteen 6, Chapter Fifteen 5, Chapter Zero 4, `cf_14.410` 4) keep it spelled out, as does "final designation under section 3" in Part A, which the rule allows; both can be swept if the custodian wants. Verification: obligation inventory for the three files, 258 and 258, matched one to one at 0.75, 0.5 and 0.3 against a snapshot taken before the edits. No duty, authority, threshold, clock, or scope changed. Translations were not touched.

**Three hundred and fortieth move (ACMD sweep across the other chapters; 2026-11-07; wording only).** Decision (custodian): "please do the sweep" of the 339th move's open item, and "it is acceptable that the body uses the abbreviation while headings, link text and glosses keep the full phrase." (1) **Rule used.** In each file, every collapsed `<details>` block and the visible body is its own reading unit. A unit that uses the full term (outside headings, link text, `(*gloss*)` text, inline code, and "In plain terms" lines) twice or more defines ACMD in parentheses at its first use and writes ACMD after that; a unit with one use keeps the full term, so no abbreviation is defined and left unused. (2) **Files changed** (30 uses converted, 13 definitions added): `core_00.000` (Preamble, 1 definition, 6 uses), `core_07.000` (1, 2), `core_11.005` (1, 1), `core_11.010` (1, 2; written "anti-constitutional-misconduct designation, ACMD" inside an existing parenthesis), `core_14.000` (1, 4), `core_15.000` (2, 2), `core_16.000` (2, 2), `cf_14.410` (1, 1), and `ci_00.210` (1, 1). The other Chapter Five entries (about eleven uses, including the owner glosses for Chapter Thirteen §2) keep the full term. (4) **Left as is.** Single-use files (`core_03`, `core_06.005`, `core_11.000`, `core_18`, `cf_14.425`, `ci_12.210`, `cjs_05.125`) and single-use units elsewhere keep the full term; the 339th move's count of other chapters (Chapter Five 15 uses and so on) is superseded by this list. **Three hundred and forty-first move (Chapter Eleven Part B §9.2.3: Educational Misconduct and Research and Discovery Misconduct cells rewritten; 2026-11-07; wording only, uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): clarify the phrase "pathway is recorded separately from the impact slot" in the two §9.2.3 cells and implement the suggested rewrites. (1) **Educational Misconduct** now reads "Harm to learning or to sentients' ability to act for themselves (their capabilities). The slot follows the total LEQU loss." (2) **Research and Discovery Misconduct** now reads "Harm to research or discovery. The slot follows the total LEQU loss." (3) **Why.** The old sentence could be read as keeping the education or research harm itself out of the slot. That wording fits the conduct-character rows (Violent, Agency and Manipulation, Negligent), where the descriptor is a conduct fact that does not move the slot under §8.2.1, but education and research harm is a kind of loss and counts in the LEQU estimate. "Pathway" is also gone from both cells, which removes confusion with the Chapter Twelve "named pathway" (a standing-lock scope). (4) **Effect on rules.** None intended: the slot already followed the total LEQU loss (§8.2, §12), and no must, should, scope, or audit rule changed. The Agency, Negligent, and Violent rows keep their "recorded separately" wording, which is correct for conduct facts. (5) **Open.** The stale copy `_pages_site/core_10.000_standing_assessment.md` line 739 still carries the old wording (a generated or archived copy, not touched). (6) **Benefit-side mirror (custodian, "please").** The Education and Capability Building row in §9.1.3 now ends "The slot follows the total LEQU benefit." to mirror the violation-side rows; no other benefit row was changed. The other §9.1 rows (for example STEM and Discovery Contribution, Remedial and restorative benefit, Resourceful Stewardship) still carry no slot sentence, so the pairing is incomplete; extending it is an open choice. Wording only; no rule changed.

**Three hundred and forty-second move (Violation Axis slot labels 5–7 reordered: Serious, Major, Severe; 2026-11-07; label change, uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): the old labels put Major (5), Severe (6), Serious (7) in an unnatural order of escalation, so slot 5 is now Serious, slot 6 Major, slot 7 Severe. Slots 1–4, 8, and 9 are unchanged (Minimal, Limited, Material, Significant; Grave, Catastrophic), and the Contribution Axis labels are unchanged. (1) **Chapter Eleven Part B.** §10.1 Table 1 and the §12 Table 2 rows for slots 5–7 carry the new violation labels. The Table 2 descriptions that echoed the old labels follow them: slot 5 "verified serious constitutional loss" (was "major"), slot 6 "major verified loss approaching one LEQU" (was "severe"; it now matches the slot 6 benefit text "major verified benefit approaching one LEQU"); slot 7 "at least one LEQU of verified constitutional loss" has no adjective and is unchanged. (2) **Chapter Thirteen.** The slot 7 row of the designation table now reads "Severe Constitutional Impact" and **"Severe anti-constitutional misconduct"** (was "Serious"), and the line naming the seriousness label reads "(Severe, Grave, or Catastrophic)". This renames the label a designation carries at slot 7; which findings qualify (slots 7, 8, 9) is unchanged. (3) **Implementation files.** `implementation/CH06_NINE_SLOT_STANDING_SCALE.md` (table and label list), `implementation/ch06_nine_slot_constants.json` (violation display strings for `s` = 5, 6, 7), `implementation/schemas/ch06_assessment.schema.json` (the `chapterTwelveLabel` enum value "Severe anti-constitutional misconduct"), `implementation/LEQU_CALIBRATION_REFERENCE.md` (W1 is now Severe, W4 Serious), and the s = 5, 6, 7 boxes in `guides/CONCEPTUAL_OVERVIEW.md`. (4) **Baseline.** `tools/time_reference_baseline.json` re-keyed three rewritten Table 2 rows (same 79 entries; nothing added). (5) **Effect on rules.** No duty, threshold, band, or LEQU boundary changed; only the display names for three slots and the one designation label. An adopted implementation or stored record that uses the old strings ("Serious anti-constitutional misconduct" in particular, which now means the slot 7 label and not slot 5) must map them, because "Serious" and "Severe" have swapped places in the numbering. (6) **Not touched.** `tools/ch06_parallel_axis_restructure.py` (a one-time migration script that quotes the old text), the stale `_pages_site` copies, and `translations/` (none contain the labels).

**Three hundred and forty-third move (Violation Axis slot labels 3 and 4 swapped: Significant, Material; 2026-11-07; label change, uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): to line up with the Contribution Axis, where slot 4 is "Material Positive Contribution", the Violation Axis slot 3 is now **Significant Constitutional Impact** and slot 4 is **Material Constitutional Impact** (they were the reverse). The full Violation order is now Minimal, Limited, Significant, Material, Serious, Major, Severe, Grave, Catastrophic. Stored records or implementations that use the old strings for slots 3 and 4 must map them, because "Material" and "Significant" have traded places in the numbering. The Contribution Axis labels are unchanged. **Three hundred and forty-fourth move (Violation Axis slot 4 label: Material becomes Substantial; 2026-11-07; label change, uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): "substantial is a better fit, because even at the lowest tier, violations are material because of the damage they do to trust." Slot 4 is now **Substantial Constitutional Impact** (the 343rd move had made it Material); the Violation order is Minimal, Limited, Significant, Substantial, Serious, Major, Severe, Grave, Catastrophic, and the Contribution Axis label "Material Positive Contribution" is unchanged. (3) **Why it also helps.** "Material" and "Material Impact" are Chapter Five defined terms (Materiality; Material Impact, the threshold that triggers classification-scaled duties); a slot label spelled "Material Constitutional Impact" invited reading the slot as that threshold. No slot label now repeats a defined term. (4) **Effect on rules.** None: display name only. Stored records or implementations using "Material Constitutional Impact" for slot 4 (the 343rd move's label) or for slot 3 (the original label) must map them. Same exclusions as the 342nd move.

**Three hundred and forty-fifth move (Violation Axis slots 3 and 4 swapped again: Substantial, Significant; 2026-11-07; label change, uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): swap Substantial and Significant. Slot 3 is now **Substantial Constitutional Impact** and slot 4 is **Significant Constitutional Impact**. The Violation order is Minimal, Limited, Substantial, Significant, Serious, Major, Severe, Grave, Catastrophic; the Contribution Axis labels are unchanged. Rationale offered in session: slots 1 to 3 use size words and slots 4 to 5 use importance words, so Significant leads into Serious. (3) **Effect on rules.** None: display names only. Slot 3 has been Material (original), Significant (343rd move), and is now Substantial; slot 4 has been Significant (original), Material (343rd move), Substantial (344th move), and is now Significant. Stored records or implementations using an earlier string for slot 3 or slot 4 must map it. Same exclusions as the 342nd move.

**Three hundred and forty-sixth move (Contribution Axis slot 4 label: Material Positive becomes Substantial Positive; 2026-11-07; label change, uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): "We want Substantial in slot 4 of the contribution axis name as well." Slot 4 on the Contribution Axis is now **Substantial Positive Contribution** (was Material Positive Contribution); no other Contribution label changed, and the C-PC band (slots 3 and 4) keeps its name. **Three hundred and forty-seventh move (Contribution Axis slots 3 to 5 renamed to match the Violation Axis words; 2026-11-07; label change, uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, "We also want the slot 3 and slot 5 to match on the contribution side as well, since there is no reason not to do that"; the custodian chose to keep the Violation Axis as it is after the 345th move). The Contribution Axis slots now use the same lead word as the Violation Axis at slots 3 to 5: slot 3 **Substantial Positive Contribution** (was Verified Positive), slot 4 **Significant Positive Contribution** (the 346th move had made it Substantial Positive; originally Material Positive), slot 5 **Serious Stewardship-Positive Contribution** (was Established Stewardship-Positive). The band names C-PC (slots 3 and 4) and C-SP (slots 5 and 6) are unchanged. **Three hundred and forty-eighth move (slots 1 and 2 renamed Minor and Small on both axes; 2026-11-07; label change, uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): use "minor" and "small" for the first two slots on both axes. Slot 1 is now **Minor Baseline Contribution** / **Minor Constitutional Impact** (was Basic Baseline / Minimal), and slot 2 is **Small Baseline Contribution** / **Small Constitutional Impact** (was Strengthened Baseline / Limited). The full scale is now: 1 Minor, 2 Small, 3 Substantial, 4 Significant, 5 Serious, 6 Major (Stewardship-Positive on the Contribution side from slot 5), 7 Severe (Violation) / Recognized Champion, 8 Grave / Distinguished Champion, 9 Catastrophic / Exemplary Champion. (2) **Implementation files.** `implementation/CH06_NINE_SLOT_STANDING_SCALE.md` (table and Violation label list), `implementation/ch06_nine_slot_constants.json` (`s` = 1 and 2, both axes), `implementation/LEQU_CALIBRATION_REFERENCE.md` (W5 now Small, W11 now Minor, and the litter-pickup standard-measure row now cites Small Baseline Contribution), and the s = 1 and s = 2 boxes in `guides/CONCEPTUAL_OVERVIEW.md`; `tools/time_reference_baseline.json` re-keyed (same entry count). Records or implementations using the earlier strings must map them. (4) **Note.** The stable interchange sub-tier key `minor` (with `mid` and `major`, which group slots 1 to 9 in threes for compatibility) is a data key, not a display label, and is unchanged; the display label "Minor" is now used at slot 1 only, while the key `minor` is the first slot of each group (slots 1, 4, and 7). The two are unrelated, so an implementation has no reason to map one to the other. Same exclusions as the 342nd move.

**Three hundred and forty-ninth move (Chapter Eleven Part B: Standard contribution measures moved from §10.2 to a new §13; 2026-11-07; layout and pointers, plus one plain-terms line).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, "B please", after the evaluation `claude/ch11-part-b-section-10-2-standard-contribution-measures-evaluation.md`; uncommitted): the former §10.2 *Standard contribution measures* in `core_11.005_standing_measurement.md` did not fit §10 (*Slot grammar and shared scaling*): it was Contribution-only inside a both-axes section, it mostly governs who may publish and revise a standard rather than how to measure, and it cited §10.3 and §12, both of which come after it. It is now its own section, **§13 Standard contribution measures**, placed after the §12 scale it relies on and before the worked illustrations. Crosswalk (old to new): 10.2 Standard contribution measures to 13; 10.3 Shared impact-scaling rules to 10.2; 13 Measurement illustrations to 14 (13.1 to 14.1, 13.2 to 14.2); 14 Hand-off to Question 3 to 15 (14.1 to 15.1); 10.1 and §1–§12 otherwise unchanged. Wording: the six bullets and the closing sentences are unchanged. The lead lost one word ("may also publish" is now "may publish", because the "also" pointed back at the 10.1 closing paragraph and the section now stands alone), its internal cite to the shared scaling rules now reads "section 10.2", and §13 gained an "In plain terms" line (a floor and not a cap, updated on a published schedule, contestable, no duty to perform). Both no-override sentences were kept on purpose: the 10.1 closing paragraph guards measurement units, evidence methods and examples, and §13's closing sentence guards standards, so after the move they are no longer neighbors saying the same thing, and dropping either would remove an express guardrail. No duty, authority, threshold, clock, or scope changed. Retargeted: Part B placement note (now §7–§15 and names standard contribution measures), its two Trace lists, the §8 plain-terms line and the two §8 tables, and the §12 use bullet; Part A placement note, sections list, Part B pointer and label (Part A §4.5's "under section 10.2" now reads "section 13"); Chapter Eleven vignettes (two links); Chapter Twelve (the §14 illustrations pointer and the 14.1 and 14.2 depth pointers); CI-6.255 and CI-15.205 (links to §13); `implementation/LEQU_CALIBRATION_REFERENCE.md` (§6.4 binding home and related-materials row, the §10.2 shared-scaling cites, and the W3 and W4 example cites). Old fragment ids were deleted, not aliased (NAV-PRE-RELEASE-FRAGMENT-01); a sweep of live files found no remaining links to `#102-standard-contribution-measures`, `#103-shared-impact-scaling-rules`, `#13-measurement-illustrations`, `#131-`, `#132-`, `#14-hand-off-` or `#141-`. Regenerated: `ai_corpus` section manifest, section crossref and crossref matrix; boundary chunks; the plain-terms edition. `make pages-site` did not run on this device because deleting files in the connected folder is blocked, and `_pages_site/` is an ignored build folder; CI rebuilds it. Hazard for later edits: the numbers 10.2, 13, 14 and 15 now mean different sections than before this move. Dated records (this log's earlier entries, `project/`, `archive/`, `evidence/`) keep the old numbering by design; translations were not touched and stay pinned to the old numbering.

**Three hundred and fiftieth move (Chapter Eleven Part B §10.2 unrealized-danger paragraph reworded for average readers; 2026-11-07; wording only, no rule changed).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, "please rewrite for average humans"; uncommitted): the closing paragraph of §10.2 Shared impact-scaling rules in `core_11.005_standing_measurement.md` now reads: "If a danger has not actually happened yet, record each of these separately: how likely it is, how long sentients would be exposed to it, how far it could reach, how uncertain the estimate is, and the worst outcome that is still believable. Do not count that worst outcome as harm that has already happened (LEQU loss) unless adopted implementation has set a risk calibration (a method for converting risk into LEQU) that anyone can inspect." Before: "For unrealized danger, record probability, exposure duration, affected scope, uncertainty, and the worst credible outcome separately. Do not treat the worst credible outcome as realized LEQU loss without an adopted, transparent risk calibration." Same five separate records, same bar for treating the worst outcome as realized loss (an adopted, open-to-inspection risk calibration); "unrealized" and "realized" became "has not actually happened yet" and "has already happened", "affected scope" became "how far it could reach", and "transparent" became "anyone can inspect", with the calibration glossed in place. No duty, authority, threshold, clock, or scope changed. The one-line paraphrase in `implementation/LEQU_CALIBRATION_REFERENCE.md` (§2) was left as is, since it points here and is process support. Follow-up (custodian, "please", offered a bulleted list): the five records are now a bulleted list (how likely it is; how long sentients would be exposed to it; how far it could reach; how uncertain the estimate is; the worst outcome that is still believable), with the lead-in line and the closing sentence unchanged; wording unchanged and nothing added. Regenerated: `ai_corpus` section manifest, section crossref and crossref matrix; boundary chunks; the plain-terms edition.

**Three hundred and fifty-first move (Chapter Eleven Part B §13–§15: bulleted and plain-language edits, a matching list of seats for Example 2, two stale cites repaired; 2026-11-07; wording and layout, plus one new list of seats; merged in PR #176).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, in this order: "please reorganize chapter 11b, section 13. Standard contribution measures. group the bullets logically, and use subbullets where appropriate"; "Bullets here please" for the Example 1 seats paragraph; "I'd like section 14.2 to more closely mirror 14.1. It think it should list seats"; "both Q2 outputs should be bullet lists"; "please rewrite for average humans" and "please clarify" for the Q2 outputs and the §15 guide; "Now add bullets please"; "I think we may want a reminder that contribution and violation records are separate here"): (1) §13: the six bullets of *Standard contribution measures* are regrouped under three headings (*How a standard applies*, *How a standard is revised*, *Safeguards against misuse and over-reach*) with sub-bullets, the floor-not-a-cap bullet nesting its two cases; wording unchanged apart from sentence splits and capitals, and the lead, the *In plain terms* line and both closing sentences untouched. (2) §14.1: the Seats paragraph is now a bulleted list under four labels (*Who is involved*, *Who holds the seats*, *Separation of the seats*, *Opening and contest*); the hosting sentence was split and lost its ", but". Its label cited "section 3.7", a section that does not exist (Part A has no §3.7; the four seats are Part A §5.1, a leftover from the record-custody move to top-level §5), and now reads "section 5.1". (3) §14.2 gained the matching list, **new text**, written from Part A §5.1, §5.2 and §5.4: the steward is the subject and holds no seat; the three cooperatives are the affected parties and the claimants; the trust board is an affected party whose copy of the submitted exports is an input and holds neither seat; the regulator's notice is an input; the verifying forum is the record-opening authority and a separate record custodian (named by the forum's designation, or the Backup Record Custodian) holds the record; no one verifies and enters, and the verifying forum does not hear the contest, which goes to secondary review. The example does not state who the claimants are or why the board holds no seat, so those two points are inferred and are flagged for the custodian's review; the list illustrates existing rules and adds none. (4) Both *Q2 outputs recorded* paragraphs are plain-language bullet lists. In Example 2 the response-delay sentence is two bullets (the steward's slow response after the regulator's notice stays a Question 2 input, recorded as its own conduct fact with any extra harm counted once; a later delay in starting or carrying out the remedy is a different Question 3 fact under Chapter Twelve §9.5, established separately), the deception bullet now says what Question 3 does with it (it may lead to a stronger standing lock under Chapter Twelve §4.2; §15 lists the hand-off; the fact is recorded here, used there, and not counted twice), and the two route names carry short glosses (*Ecological Misconduct* "contaminating the watershed", *Negligent Misconduct* "failing a maintenance duty"). (5) §15: the *Cross-question guide* is rewritten in plain language as a lead sentence and bullets, with a reminder, linked to Part A §2.4, that a Contribution record and a Violation record are always measured separately and one never offsets the other; its pointer "Sections 4.1–4.4" (stale since the Part A and B split; those Part A sections cover record contents) now reads "Sections 8 and 9"; "feature" became "fact" in the guide only (the table header still says "feature"). No duty, authority, threshold, clock, or scope changed. CI caught one slip: the two "Seats under section 5.1." labels had to end in a colon (MD-LIST-INTRO-01); fixed in the same pull request. This entry was written late, in the pull request that adds PREFLIGHT-01, because the session had skipped MOVE-LOG-AUTO-01; the same pull request regenerates the `ai_corpus` section manifest, section crossref and crossref matrix, which were stale after these edits.

**Three hundred and fifty-second move (Git workflow notes: pushing and merging from a Claude Code session, and resyncing after a replayed pull request; 2026-11-07; documentation only, one rule extended; merged in PRs #177, #178 and #179).** Decision (custodian, asked in session: "make a note of how you pushed for the future in agents.md"; "Don't we have a note to keep the local branch up to date somewhere? If not, please add it"; "please make the wording change"): (1) `AGENTS.md` gained *Pushing and merging from a Claude Code session*: the shell on the custodian's computer has no GitHub credentials and no `gh`, so the push goes through the Claude Code workspace (attach the repo with push access, replay the unpushed commits from `git format-patch` files staged out of `_to_delete/`, push, open the pull request with `gh api` because `gh pr create` needs GraphQL, which is blocked, wait for the `regression` job and compare its failures with the last run on `main`, merge with a merge commit), plus the stale `.git/*.lock` workaround. It applies only when the custodian asks for a push or merge. (2) GIT-LOCAL-SYNC-01 already covered fetching, a pure-mirror `main`, and leaving a merged branch; it did not cover a pull request made by replaying local commits, where `origin/main` holds the same changes under new hashes and GitHub deletes the merged branch. It now says not to merge `origin/main` into the old branch and to run `git fetch origin main:main` and `git cherry -v origin/main <branch>` (a `-` line is already in `main`, a `+` line is local work to carry to a new branch), then delete the old branch; the rule-table row and `AGENTS.md` step 6 say the same. This adds one "do not" and one procedure to an existing rule; nothing else changed. (3) the new text first named the remote workspace with a word the lexical vocabulary audit forbids; it now says "Claude Code workspace" throughout, in `AGENTS.md` and in the rule text.

**Three hundred and fifty-third move (`make preflight`, PREFLIGHT-01, and a "Before you report done" checklist at the top of `AGENTS.md`; 2026-11-07; one new make target, one new rule, one new flag; uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, "Do you think it might be time for something like a rule index? Something could be optimized if we're having issues with rules not being read or used consistently", then "please" to the offer to build `make preflight` and a trigger table first): the finding was that the rules exist but are not triggered at the moment they matter. `AGENTS.md` already told an agent to run `make audit-report` (AUDIT-AUTOFIX-01) and to add a move-log entry (MOVE-LOG-AUTO-01), and the session still skipped both until CI and the custodian's question exposed it. So: (1) `tools/audit_report.py` gains `--preflight` (`make preflight`): the 77 read-only gate audits, minus the 48 unit tests of the audit scripts and the one file-writing target, failures only, with no refresh of the AI indexes; about a minute here, against about a minute and a half for the full report, because `section-label-anchor-audit` and `lexical-vocabulary-audit` dominate both. A test covers the selection and the flag. (2) PREFLIGHT-01 is added to the rule registry and `make preflight` to the audit command table. Preflight is a convenience and not a gate; AUDIT-SCOPE-01 is unchanged. (3) `AGENTS.md` gains a four-item *Before you report done* checklist near the top (run preflight and the full report, add the move-log entry, regenerate generated files, follow the git rules), each item naming its rule. (4) The `ai_corpus` section manifest, section crossref and crossref matrix are regenerated. They were stale on `main`: CI refreshes them silently before it checks, so it never failed, and the corpus edits of the 351st move had not been regenerated. Also fixed: `audit_report.py --changed` used to fall back to the full gate whenever any gate target lacked an `audit_scope_registry.json` entry (42 did, 40 of them the `-test` targets, which the scope probe leaves out on purpose), and then printed "non-corpus changes are present" whatever the reason. When only corpus files changed it now skips the unit tests of the audits and keeps any other unregistered audit on its own; it falls back to the full set only when a non-corpus file changed (tools, the Makefile), and says so. On a one-file corpus edit it selects 49 of 126 targets, but the two slowest audits are in that set, so the run takes about as long as `make preflight` (about a minute here); two tests cover the selection. Repaired in the same pull request under AUDIT-AUTOFIX-01: RECORD-KIND-01, the only gate audit red on `main`, flagged "verified record" in the Contribution Nature row of the §11.1 table in `core_11.005_standing_measurement.md` (line 481); it now reads "verified contribution record", the wording Chapter Five's *Contribution Nature* entry uses, so the gate is green again. Corrected: earlier notes in this session called TIME-LOCK-01 a second red audit, and that was wrong. Those lines in the CI log come from `tools/test_time_window_lockstep_audit.py`, which prints failure messages on purpose and passes; the `AGENTS.md` push note (step 5) now says to read the `Result: FAIL (n gate targets failing)` line and the failing target names, not every `FAIL` line. A rule index generated from the audit registry is the next step if the checklist does not hold. No duty, authority, threshold, clock, or scope in the corpus changed.

**Three hundred and fifty-fourth move (keeping the custodian's checkout current by default: `make sync-local`, bundle transfer, a session-start fetch, and VS Code and git settings; 2026-11-07; one new script, one rule extended; uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, "I would prefer my local machine be kept up to date. What do we need to do to make that the default?", then "please implement your suggestions, including the vs code config"): the finding was that the shell on the custodian's computer can fetch, because the repository is public (only pushing needs credentials), which the session had assumed it could not; the real obstacles were commits replayed from patches under new hashes, so the local branch looked diverged after the merge, and the deletion approval that updating files there needs. So: (1) `tools/sync_local.py` and `make sync-local`: fetch with `--prune`, fast-forward `main`, leave and delete a merged branch the checkout is on, and list other branches whose remote is gone, never deleting one with a commit missing from `origin/main` (compared by `git cherry`, so replayed commits still count as merged), never switching with uncommitted tracked changes, never touching untracked files; `--dry-run`, `--delete-merged` and `--no-fetch` are options. Eight tests against throwaway repositories run inside `make audit-report-test`. (2) The push note in `AGENTS.md` now transfers commits as a git bundle, which keeps their hashes (round trip tested: a shallow clone must be deepened first so the bundle's base commit is present), instead of replaying `git format-patch` files; its last step is `make sync-local`. (3) `AGENTS.md` gained item 5 of the *Before you report done* checklist: a dry run at the start of a session, `make sync-local` after a merge, and one deletion approval per session. (4) `make install-hooks` also sets `fetch.prune true` and `pull.ff only`; both were set in the custodian's checkout directly. (5) The VS Code workspace settings listed in GIT-LOCAL-SYNC-01 were written into the custodian's local `.vscode/settings.json` (the existing `git.ignoreLimitWarning` was kept); the file is not tracked, so it is not in the commit. (6) GIT-LOCAL-SYNC-01 text and table row now name `make sync-local` and keep the replayed-commit case as a fallback; this adds one "run" step and no new prohibition. Done in the session: the custodian's checkout was fast-forwarded to `main` after pull request #180 and the merged `lane-d/ch11-standing-record-restructure` branch was deleted; 51 other local branches whose remote is gone remain, listed by `sync-local` and not deleted. No duty, authority, threshold, clock, or scope in the corpus changed.

**Three hundred and fifty-fifth move (a standing note for the custodian: run `make sync-local` on your own computer after each merge; 2026-11-08; one sentence added in two places; uncommitted).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, "please make a note to run `make sync-local` on my mac after each merge"): the existing notes told the session to run `make sync-local` after a merge but did not tell the custodian, so a merge made while no session was linked to the computer would leave the checkout behind. So: (1) the *What to do* list in GIT-LOCAL-SYNC-01 gained a bullet, *On the custodian's own computer: run `make sync-local` after each merge*, saying when to run it, that it is safe at any time, and that a session which could not run it says so in its final reply; (2) item 5 of the *Before you report done* checklist in `AGENTS.md` now ends with that duty: if the computer was not reachable, the final reply tells the custodian to run `make sync-local`. No new script and no change to the rule table row. No duty, authority, threshold, clock, or scope in the corpus changed.
